natural-order ladders and finite monomial weights, while
\nolinkurl{FabiusFractionalVolterra.lean} gives the positive-real one-step
fractional shifts for the signed extension on \(x\geq0\), the bounded function
on \([0,1]\), and the bridge from \(\RvachevUp\) based at \(-1\) to \(F\).

\subsubsection{Path E: sharp asymptotics}
Read:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \nolinkurl{FabiusLogScale.lean};
  \item \nolinkurl{FabiusLogSquaredAsymptotic.lean};
  \item \nolinkurl{NegativeLaplace.lean};
  \item \nolinkurl{PeriodicCorrection.lean};
  \item \nolinkurl{MellinBose.lean};
  \item \nolinkurl{FabiusLambertPhase.lean};
  \item \nolinkurl{BromwichSaddle.lean};
  \item \nolinkurl{FabiusSharpExactReduction.lean};
  \item \nolinkurl{FabiusSharpAsymptotic.lean}; and
  \item \nolinkurl{FabiusFullAsymptoticExpansion.lean}.
\end{enumerate}
Use the specialized central/tail and all-order modules as dependencies are encountered.

\subsection{Adding a new exact identity}
Suppose a new conjectured formula concerns \(F(a/2^n)\).  A productive workflow is:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item state and test the identity entirely in \(\RationalNumbers\) using \nolinkurl{fabiusDyadicValue};
  \item normalize representations and determine whether the bounded or global extension is intended;
  \item prove the finite arithmetic identity, preferably through existing closed-form or q-binomial lemmas;
  \item only then transport it to the analytic function using dyadic correctness; and
  \item add a paper-facing wrapper if the notation comes from an external source.
\end{enumerate}
This approach keeps exploratory computation cheap and avoids starting with real analysis when the content is combinatorial.

\subsection{Adding a new moment theorem}
For a new moment recurrence:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item derive the analytic recurrence in a generic \nolinkurl{IsFabius} context;
  \item define an exact rational sequence only if computation or arithmetic consequences justify it;
  \item prove equality by strong induction;
  \item design a division-free numerator recurrence before attempting parity or divisibility; and
  \item separate reduced-denominator conclusions into a later module.
\end{enumerate}
The existing normalized moment files are templates for common-denominator induction.

\subsection{Adding an asymptotic correction}
A proposed correction term should be attached to the exact saddle reduction rather than inferred from numerical fitting.  The robust route is:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item identify which cumulant or periodic Fourier coefficient contributes at the desired order;
  \item extend the formal coefficient recurrence;
  \item prove a uniform central remainder at that order;
  \item prove the tail is smaller than the target scale;
  \item update the normalized mass expansion; and
  \item transfer through logarithm and the exact phase.
\end{enumerate}
If the desired final formula is in elementary logarithms, derive it as a corollary of the exact-phase theorem using \nolinkurl{FabiusLambertAllOrderSmallArgument.lean}.

\subsection{Testing a suspected source error}
Formalization is most efficient when the claim is stress-tested early.
\begin{itemize}
  \item Check endpoint and smallest-index cases by exact evaluation.
  \item Verify domains of every differentiated functional equation.
  \item Compare bounded and signed-global versions under translation.
  \item For asymptotic claims, sample subsequences of fixed dyadic phase.
  \item For uniform errors, examine knots and support boundaries.
  \item For q-dependent finite rows, test nondyadic locations before claiming parameter independence.
\end{itemize}
A small counterexample theorem is often more informative than a long stalled proof attempt.

\subsection{Maintaining dependency hygiene}
Before importing the umbrella module into a new file, ask which interface is actually needed.  An exact lemma should normally depend on \nolinkurl{Arithmetic.lean} or a narrow q-binomial module, not on \nolinkurl{Existence.lean}.  A generic analytic lemma should depend on \nolinkurl{Basic.lean} and the relevant calculus modules, not on the canonical instance unless the theorem truly uses it.

A useful check is to run the import-graph tooling included through mathlib's dependencies.  Unexpected paths from asymptotic modules into paper aggregators or from arithmetic modules into analysis are signs that a helper theorem belongs in a lower layer.

\subsection{Naming and theorem granularity}
The repository's long module names encode the proof stage: \nolinkurl{Raw}, \nolinkurl{Scalar}, \nolinkurl{ExactReduction}, \nolinkurl{Transfer}, \nolinkurl{Central}, \nolinkurl{Tail}, \nolinkurl{AllOrders}, and so on.  New declarations should preserve this vocabulary where possible.

A theorem should expose one conceptual transition.  Good boundaries include:
\begin{itemize}
  \item closed formula equals Horner program;
  \item analytic recurrence equals exact recurrence;
  \item central integral equals Gaussian main term plus error;
  \item exact phase equals truncated elementary expansion plus error; and
  \item source statement follows from the stronger internal theorem.
\end{itemize}
Large proofs become navigable when these transitions have names.

\subsection{A minimal downstream example}
A downstream file that only needs exact evaluation and the analytic correctness theorem can begin as follows:
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction.PaperStatements

open Fabius

example (n : Nat) (a : Int) :
    (fabiusDyadicValue n a : Real) =
      fabiusReal fabius ((a : Real) / (2 : Real) ^ n) := by
  exact fabiusDyadicValue_cast fabius fabius_spec n a
\end{lstlisting}
This statement accepts every signed numerator, so no reduced-fraction or
unit-interval side condition remains.  Importing \nolinkurl{FabiusFunction.lean}
would also work, but would hide which layer supplies the public wrapper.

\subsection{What to trust when prose and code differ}
The kernel-checked theorem type is the final authority.  Module headers and README tables explain intent and source correspondence, while the proof term certifies the exact statement.  When a paper formula differs from a Lean wrapper, inspect:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item whether the paper silently assumes an index is positive;
  \item whether a removable singularity has been totalized;
  \item whether a global extension replaces a bounded function;
  \item whether endpoint representatives differ only almost everywhere; and
  \item whether an asymptotic periodic term has been omitted.
\end{enumerate}
The repository usually records the reason in the aggregator header or a nearby correction theorem.

\section{Concluding perspective}
The ProveIt development is best understood not as a very long proof of a single theorem, but as a small mathematical library centered on one function.  It has several independent foundations:
\begin{itemize}
  \item an exact arithmetic language of moments, binary signs, products, and dyadic evaluators;
  \item an abstract analytic interface capturing the Fabius differential and symmetry laws;
  \item a Banach fixed-point construction of the bounded solution;
  \item a compact-support/Fourier theory of the Rvachev twin; and
  \item a transform-and-saddle theory for the flat endpoint.
\end{itemize}
The strongest parts of the architecture are the bridges.  The dyadic bridge identifies executable rationals with analytic values.  The moment bridge identifies finite recurrences with integrals.  The Fourier bridge identifies a refinement equation with an infinite product.  The saddle bridge identifies that product with a corrected sharp asymptotic.  The Legendre bridge turns exact moments into optimal polynomial approximants.

Formalization also changes the mathematical product.  It forces endpoint conventions, domains of delay equations, transform normalizations, and bounded-versus-global distinctions to be stated.  It exposes false residual estimates and missing terms, while often supplying a corrected theorem stronger than the source statement.  The result is not merely a verification of known facts: it is a coherent, reusable account of how the facts fit together.

For a reader entering the source, the single most useful habit is to ask which side of the project a declaration belongs to: exact arithmetic, generic analysis, canonical construction, or a bridge.  Once that question is answered, the long file list resolves into a small number of repeated proof patterns and a remarkably systematic dependency graph.

\appendix

\section{Detailed dependency map}\label{app:dependency}

\subsection{Core spine}
The shortest conceptual spine from definitions to the canonical function is:
\[
\begin{gathered}
\boxed{\text{\texttt{Arithmetic.lean}}}
\longrightarrow
\boxed{\text{\texttt{Basic.lean}}}
\longrightarrow
\boxed{\text{\texttt{Differential.lean}}}\\[1mm]
\longrightarrow
\boxed{\text{\texttt{DyadicAnalytic.lean}}}
\longrightarrow
\boxed{\text{\texttt{Existence.lean}}}\\[1mm]
\longrightarrow
\boxed{\text{\texttt{PaperStatements.lean}}}.
\end{gathered}
\]
This display omits two important side inputs.  \nolinkurl{DyadicClosedForm.lean} supplies the exact finite polynomial matched by \nolinkurl{DyadicAnalytic.lean}, while \nolinkurl{DyadicCorrectness.lean} supplies evaluator correctness and representation invariance.  \nolinkurl{Existence.lean} proves unique existence; \nolinkurl{PaperStatements.lean} makes the canonical choice and publishes \nolinkurl{fabius_spec}.  The resulting canonical function is then consumed by moment, Fourier, probability, global-extension, and asymptotic modules.

\subsection{Arithmetic spine}
The main arithmetic chain is:
\[
\begin{gathered}
\text{\texttt{Arithmetic.lean}}
\longrightarrow
\left\{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{\texttt{NormalizedMoments.lean}}\\
\text{\texttt{NormalizedEvenMoments.lean}}\\
\text{\texttt{Parity.lean}}
\end{array}
\right.\\[2mm]
\longrightarrow
\left\{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{\texttt{HalfMomentDenominator.lean}}\\
\text{\texttt{TwoAdic.lean}}\\
\text{\texttt{DenominatorBound.lean}}
\end{array}
\right.
\longrightarrow
\text{\texttt{PaperStatements.lean}}.
\end{gathered}
\]
\nolinkurl{AnalyticMoments.lean} and \nolinkurl{ExactInversePower.lean} connect this chain to the canonical function before the source-facing wrappers are assembled.

\subsection{Original-paper spine}
The original Rvachev paper is represented by two parallel paths:
\[
\begin{array}{c}
\text{differential/refinement law}
\to\text{Fourier recurrence}
\to\text{sinc product}
\to\text{uniqueness},\\[1mm]
\text{finite elementary laws}
\to\text{convolutions}
\to\text{weak convergence}
\to\text{step approximants}.
\end{array}
\]
Their principal modules are \nolinkurl{OriginalUniqueness.lean}, \nolinkurl{FourierProduct.lean}, \nolinkurl{ProbabilityRepresentation.lean}, \nolinkurl{WeakConvergence.lean}, and \nolinkurl{StepApproximationLimit.lean}.  \nolinkurl{OriginalPaperSupplement.lean} adds the remaining numbered theorems and prose claims.

\subsection{Activation spine}
The activation development consists of one local Taylor branch and a small
square-summable fork-and-join graph:
\[
\begin{gathered}
\text{\texttt{HyperbolicActivation}}
\longrightarrow
\text{\texttt{ActivationTaylor}},\\[2mm]
\text{\texttt{HyperbolicActivation}}
\longrightarrow
\left\{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{\texttt{ActivationSeries}}\\
\text{\texttt{ActivationAsymptotics}}
\end{array}
\right.\\[2mm]
\left\{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{\texttt{ActivationSeries}}\\
\text{\texttt{ActivationAsymptotics}}
\end{array}
\right.
\longrightarrow
\text{\texttt{ActivationSeriesAsymptotics}},\\[2mm]
\text{\texttt{ActivationSeries}}
\longrightarrow
\text{\texttt{GeometricActivationDimension}},\\[2mm]
\left\{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{\texttt{ActivationSeriesAsymptotics}}\\
\text{\texttt{GeometricActivationDimension}}
\end{array}
\right.
\longrightarrow
\text{\texttt{GeometricActivationAsymptotics}}.
\end{gathered}
\]
The Taylor branch is a local analytic development and terminates on its own.
Of the remaining two branches, the first contains global comparison and
summability and the second contains the one-coordinate punctured limit.
Tannery joins those two interfaces only after both are available.  Geometric
algebra stays in the structural module, so the final asymptotic specialization
contains almost no repeated series computation.

\subsection{Asymptotic spine}
The sharp endpoint proof has the following high-level flow:
\[
\begin{gathered}
\text{\texttt{AnalyticMoments.lean}}
\to\text{\texttt{NegativeLaplace.lean}}
\to\left\{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{\texttt{PeriodicCorrection.lean}}\\
\text{\texttt{MellinBose.lean}}
\end{array}
\right.\\
\to\text{\texttt{BromwichSaddle.lean}}
\to\text{\texttt{FabiusLambertPhase.lean}}\\
\to\left\{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{central modules}\\
\text{tail modules}
\end{array}
\right.
\to\text{\texttt{FabiusSharpExactReduction.lean}}\\
\to\text{\texttt{FabiusSharpAsymptotic.lean}}
\to\text{\texttt{FabiusFullAsymptoticExpansion.lean}}.
\end{gathered}
\]
The actual source graph is finer because the all-order proof separates formal coefficient algebra, small-argument Lambert expansion, Gaussian contraction, central uniformity, tail flatness, and transfer lemmas.

\subsection{How to use the map}
The declaration named after a source theorem is normally the best citation target in downstream formal work.  The principal proof location is the best learning target.  When the two differ, the wrapper is preserving source notation while the internal file proves a stronger or more reusable theorem.

Formal arbitrary-power recurrence &
\nolinkurl{Fabius.coeff_recurrence_of_mul_derivative_eq},
\nolinkurl{Fabius.mul_derivative_fallingSeries_subst_sub_one},
\nolinkurl{Fabius.coeff_fallingSeries_subst_sub_one_recurrence} & Exhaustive
0+3 \nolinkurl{UnitSeriesPowerRecurrence.lean} surface.  Multiplying
\(FG'=\beta F'G\) by \(X\), extracting the \(n\)-th Euler-derivative
coefficient, and isolating the \(j=0\) term gives
\(na_0c_n=\sum_{j=1}^n((\beta+1)j-n)a_jc_{n-j}\) over every commutative
ring.  Formal substitution in the falling-factorial series gives the
unit-constant power specialization over a rational algebra.  Thus the power
clause of \nolinkurl{alg:merged-exp-log-power} is Exact, with no analytic
branch claim.  The 204-row coefficient-calculus concordance is 63 Lean,
32 Partial, and 109 None. \\

\begin{boxedremark}
The table maps notation to declarations; it does not assert that every
informal formula in either source paper is a proved theorem.  In particular,
\nolinkurl{reshetnikovQuestion} and \nolinkurl{conjecture_sixteen} record open
assertions, the lower-Lambert coordinate is not claimed to solve the full
residual phase's exact critical equation, the periodic correction is not
constant, finite complex-\(q\) approximants need not be translation
independent, and the literal k-fold polygonal limit is formally refuted.
\end{boxedremark}

\section{Annotated module guide}\label{app:modules}

The following guide groups the principal files by role.  The development contains many fine-grained helper modules; the descriptions emphasize the transition each module contributes rather than every local lemma.

\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.31\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.63\textwidth}}
\toprule
Module & Role in the development \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
Module & Role in the development \\
\midrule
\endhead
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textbf{Core interfaces and construction}}\\[0.25em]
\nolinkurl{Arithmetic.lean} & Exact natural/rational sequences, binary weight, Thue--Morse signs, product normalizations, inverse-power values, and executable dyadic evaluators. \\
\nolinkurl{Basic.lean} & The unit-interval codomain, bounded-function type, abstract IsFabius specification, up-function, signed extension names, transforms, moments, and generating functions. \\
\nolinkurl{Differential.lean} & Derivative identities for any IsFabius candidate, including iterated scale formulas and reflected branches. \\
\nolinkurl{Existence.lean} & Banach fixed-point construction of \nolinkurl{Existence.boundedCandidate}, calculus bootstrap, unique existence, and generic equality of all \nolinkurl{IsFabius} candidates.  The canonical choice itself is made in \nolinkurl{PaperStatements.lean}. \\
\nolinkurl{ScaleTranslation.lean} & Reusable chain-rule formulas for affine rescaling and translation of Fabius and up-functions. \\
\nolinkurl{GlobalExtension.lean} & Local finiteness, smoothness, unit-interval agreement, and translation laws of the signed global extension. \\
\nolinkurl{NormalizedVolterra.lean} & Base-point and endpoint-order independent repeated integration.  Over arbitrary real normed targets it provides direct normalized kernels, division-free affine covariance for every scale (and an inverse form at nonzero scale), arbitrary base-point shifts into a local tail plus finite jet, the zero-local-tail specialization, and finite Hasse-polynomial and monomial commutators.  Completeness is confined to the continuous literal-iterator/strict-FTC bridge, derivative cancellation and smoothness, and Taylor reconstruction. \\
\nolinkurl{FractionalVolterra.lean} & Total Gamma-normalized real-order Volterra operator, unconditional endpoint/locality/scalar laws, the order-one and positive-natural-order bridges, and positive-order kernel integrability and input additivity on ordered intervals. \\
\nolinkurl{FractionalVolterraSemigroup.lean} & Shifted beta-kernel integrability and evaluation, and addition of two positive real orders for continuous Banach-valued inputs on ordered compact intervals. \\
\nolinkurl{FractionalVolterraCalculus.lean} & Positive-scale affine covariance at arbitrary real order on ordered endpoints, its inverse-smul form, and the positive-order integration-by-parts law raising the order of a right interior derivative, including the exact boundary-free specialization. \\
\nolinkurl{GlobalDyadic.lean} & Exact dyadic identities for the signed extension with unrestricted and nonreduced representatives. \\
\nolinkurl{DyadicDerivativeFiltration.lean} & Zero definitions and six theorems: support vanishing, the above-depth zero and critical-depth signed value, exact denominator-depth detection, and the all-real and dyadic below-depth formulas through \nolinkurl{extendedFabius}. \\
\addlinespace[0.45em]
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textbf{Global shape, inversion, and computability}}\\[0.25em]
\nolinkurl{ScalingRecurrence.lean} & Mathlib-only chain rules for arbitrary positive dilations, in vector and scalar forms, with caller-normalized outgoing weight; inert-parameter variants and dyadic wrappers used by negative-Laplace derivatives. \\
\nolinkurl{Monotonicity.lean} & Strict increase of \(F\) on \([0,1]\), endpoint range, and order consequences. \\
\nolinkurl{Regularity.lean} & Optimal global Lipschitz constants for \(F\) and \(\RvachevUp\). \\
\nolinkurl{Convexity.lean} & Exact second-derivative formula and sign/zero loci, convexity before the midpoint, and concavity afterward. \\
\nolinkurl{GlobalBounds.lean} & Sharp value and iterated-derivative bounds for the signed global extension and \(\RvachevUp\), including attainment and derivative-support control. \\
\nolinkurl{BoundedDerivatives.lean} & Exact global sup-norm bounds for every derivative, including attainment. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusAntiderivatives.lean} & Complete natural-order primitive ladder for the signed extension at every real endpoint, its bounded specialization on \(x\le1\), and finite natural-monomial formulas for both. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusFractionalVolterra.lean} & Positive-real one-step fractional order shift for the signed extension on \(x\ge0\), its bounded specialization on \([0,1]\), and the affine bridge from \(\RvachevUp\) based at \(-1\) to the bounded Fabius function for \(x\ge-1\). \\
\nolinkurl{EffectiveFlatness.lean} & Explicit power bounds at the flat endpoint. \\
\nolinkurl{SharpFlatness.lean} & Factorial-strengthened endpoint power bounds for \(F\) and \(\RvachevUp\). \\
\nolinkurl{NowhereAnalytic.lean} & Exact real-analytic loci: off the closed transition supports and nowhere on them. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusInverse.lean} & Totalized inverse, order isomorphism on the unit interval, exact positive reciprocal derivative, exact global smoothness locus (continuity everywhere, but differentiability and every positive finite or \(C^\infty\) order exactly off the clamping endpoints), reciprocal-cubic second derivative, midpoint jet and interior curvature loci, strict closed-half curvature, and endpoint quotient/derivative blow-up. \\
\nolinkurl{InverseModulus.lean} & Exhaustive one-definition/thirty-one-theorem structural inverse-modulus layer: global weak and maximal strict fixed-length increment shape, exact endpoint minimizers, constrained-superadditivity equality, unit-interval inverse-gap rigidity, global sharp modulus bounds and attained suprema, and effective injectivity.  The inverse equality loci are deliberately not global because of the clamped tails. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusInverseEffectiveContinuity.lean} & Exhaustive two-definition/twelve-theorem effective inverse-continuity layer: primitive-recursive factorial and report denominators, recurrence and endpoint-mass lower bounds, strict and closed global inverse moduli, and \nolinkurl{EffectivelyUniformContinuous}; the inverse remains noncomputable and no sequential realizer or combined computable-function theorem is supplied. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusInverseLogarithmicModulus.lean} & Three definitions and fifteen theorems: the primitive-recursive least binary-length order, its exact \nolinkurl{Nat.log2} formula and minimality, factorial and Delta denominators at that order, strict/closed-input reciprocal moduli, and an effective-uniform-continuity theorem for either witness; no exact endpoint-mass ceiling, tolerant-bisection realizer, sequential computability, combined computable-real-function theorem, or input-bit asymptotic. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusInverseExactDyadicModulus.lean} & Exactly two definitions and ten theorems: the positive primitive-recursive exact endpoint-mass ceiling denominator, its rational bound and arithmetic leastness, its strict inverse modulus, the endpoint counterexample for every smaller positive denominator, fixed-dyadic strict-modulus leastness, and the primitive-recursive logarithmic \(1/n\) witness with \(d(0)=1\).  Leastness is only for the fixed dyadic target; no leastness for \(1/n\), zero-input modulus conclusion, or input-bit asymptotic is asserted. \\
\nolinkurl{EffectiveMonotoneInverse.lean} & Exactly two public definitions and six theorems: sequential computability on a set, the computable unit clamp, the difference certificate and its safe-update and inconclusive-success consequences, fixed-depth dyadic tolerant-bisection correctness, and the abstract unit-interval inversion theorem.  The abstract theorem consumes a positive computable inverse modulus. \\
\nolinkurl{EffectiveGapInverse.lean} & Exactly eight public declarations: effective uniform continuity on a set; a computable positive rational sequence, its value and reciprocal denominator with specification; the rational-gap inverse modulus; unit-interval sequential computability plus effective continuity; and the total computability of the clamped extension. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusInverseComputable.lean} & Zero public definitions and exactly one theorem, \nolinkurl{fabiusInv_isComputableRealFunction}: the total inverse preserves computable real sequences by clamping and tolerant bisection and has the logarithmic-Delta effective continuity modulus. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusInverseQuarterJet.lean} & Full smooth derivative jet of the centered inverse at \(5/72\), identified with the factorial-scaled coefficients of the quadratic compositional inverse; this is not a local analytic identity. \\
\nolinkurl{ImplicitPowerSeries.lean} & Nonmonic complete-adic lifting and residue-class uniqueness, with arbitrary-base and zero-constant formal implicit-root specializations over power-series rings; it does not construct or analytically realize a Fabius inverse germ. \\
\nolinkurl{QuarterCatalanGerm.lean} & The explicit scaled quarter Catalan formal germ, its functional equation and uniqueness, and the forward/reverse rescaling bridge from \nolinkurl{dyadicGermTwo} to \nolinkurl{QuadraticInverse.inverse}. \\
\nolinkurl{InverseBranch.lean} & Analytic inverse theorem, analytic-density preservation for continuous right-inverse branches, and the complement-interior analyticity condition and conditional Lambert-branch criterion for \nolinkurl{IsElementaryOrInverse}. \\
\nolinkurl{AlgebraicBranch.lean} & Algebraic-function branch interface used in non-elementarity arguments. \\
\nolinkurl{ElementaryFunction.lean} & Formal expression language and semantics for elementary real functions. \\
\nolinkurl{NotElementary.lean} & Non-elementarity theorem for the bounded Fabius function on its transition interval. \\
\nolinkurl{InverseNotElementary.lean} & Interior noncriticality derived from the positive derivative API in \nolinkurl{FabiusInverse.lean}, the exact analytic locus of \nolinkurl{fabiusInv}, and local analytic-density/non-elementarity obstructions. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusComputability.lean} & Global uniform spline error \(\leq2^{-p}\), moving-input stability and diagonal convergence, an effective uniform-continuity modulus, and the computable-real-function bridge. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusComputableSpline.lean} & Primitive-recursive bounded and signed-global dyadic spline evaluators, each with an explicit error certificate, and their computable-real interfaces. \\
\addlinespace[0.45em]
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textbf{Dyadic formulas and verified computation}}\\[0.25em]
\nolinkurl{DyadicClosedForm.lean} & Closed finite Taylor formulas, highest-bit decompositions, and equivalence among exact dyadic expressions. \\
\nolinkurl{DyadicCorrectness.lean} & Termination-facing invariants, table-prefix stability, Horner correctness, refinement invariance, and evaluator representation independence. \\
\nolinkurl{DyadicAnalytic.lean} & Finite Taylor recurrence for an abstract IsFabius function and proof that analytic dyadic values equal the exact evaluator. \\
\nolinkurl{ExactInversePower.lean} & Links inverse-power Fabius values with exact moments and table entries. \\
\nolinkurl{TaylorReduction.lean} & Finite Taylor-reduction identities used to move between dyadic cells. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusDyadicLogBounds.lean} & Explicit logarithmic bounds for inverse-dyadic values, used in endpoint asymptotics. \\
\nolinkurl{DyadicSharpConditional.lean} & Conditional sharp statements at dyadic points under specified recurrence estimates. \\
\nolinkurl{DyadicSharpDecomposition.lean} & Exact decomposition of dyadic logarithms into main and correction pieces. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusDyadicSharpCumulant.lean} & Cumulant organization specialized to inverse-dyadic asymptotics. \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusDyadicSharpAsymptotic.lean} & Sharp asymptotic statements along the inverse-power sequence. \\
\addlinespace[0.45em]
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textbf{Moments, arithmetic, and denominators}}\\[0.25em]
\nolinkurl{AnalyticMoments.lean} & Compact-support integration, total mass, moment and half-moment recurrences, identification with exact rational sequences, and the named even Fourier modes \nolinkurl{rvachevFourierMomentTerm} with their zero term, scaling law, and genuine \nolinkurl{HasSum} to the Rvachev Fourier transform. \\
\nolinkurl{MomentPowerSeries.lean} & Exact \nolinkurl{PowerSeries} algebra over \(\RationalNumbers\), including \(M(4X)=M(X)S(X)\); analytic evaluation is deferred to later modules. \\
\nolinkurl{FinitePrefixAppellRecovery.lean} & Exhaustive 11+17 rational finite-prefix Appell API named in the crosswalk: constructive digit-moment convolutions, exact uncentered and centered-even expansions, exact symbolic outer degrees, and finite geometric-Lagrange recovery.  Specializing the inner variable can lower the centered outer degree; no analytic MGF convergence is asserted. \\
\nolinkurl{NormalizedMoments.lean} & Division-free normalization theorem for half moments using factorial and Mersenne products. \\
\nolinkurl{NormalizedEvenMoments.lean} & Natural numerator and common-denominator formula for even centered moments. \\
\nolinkurl{Parity.lean} & Lucas-theorem parity analysis and oddness of natural moment numerators. \\
\nolinkurl{TwoAdic.lean} & Reduced numerator/denominator parity and exact two-adic valuation consequences. \\
\nolinkurl{PrimePowerBinomialValuation.lean} & No definitions and six exhaustive theorems for prime-power Pascal rows: additive/subtraction forms for \(0<j\le p^m\), the unit row \(p^m-1\), the exact companion \(\nu_p\binom{p^m-2}{j-1}=\nu_p(j)\) for \(0<j<p^m\), and both dyadic wrappers; no histogram result. \\
\nolinkurl{DenominatorBound.lean} & Common-denominator divisibility and explicit denominator bounds for source-facing arithmetic results. \\
\nolinkurl{HalfMomentDenominator.lean} & Denominator control specialized to half moments and inverse-power values. \\
\nolinkurl{BernoulliRecurrences.lean} & Translation of moment recurrences into Bernoulli-number identities used by the arithmetic paper. \\
\nolinkurl{LaplaceMoments.lean} & Moment identities in the Laplace-transform normalization. \\
\nolinkurl{LaplaceMomentBounds.lean} & Uniform factorial/exponential bounds needed for transform series and differentiation. \\
\nolinkurl{ProbabilityLaplaceMoments.lean} & Moment and Laplace identities connecting the weighted-sum probability model with the analytic generating function; its two newest theorems, \nolinkurl{weightedSumDistribution_real_Ici_eq_rvachevUp_of_nonneg} and \nolinkurl{integral_pow_weightedSumDistribution_eq_mul_intervalIntegral_rvachevUp}, identify the atomless closed tail with \(\RvachevUp\) on the nonnegative ray and give the full-law positive-natural-degree tail-moment formula, making \nolinkurl{prop:up-tail} and \nolinkurl{cor:up-moments} Exact for \(t\geq0\) and natural \(n\geq1\). \\
\nolinkurl{FabiusRecurrenceSequence.lean} & Auxiliary exact recurrence sequence used in asymptotic and dyadic estimates. \\
\addlinespace[0.45em]
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textbf{Fourier, probability, and the first paper}}\\[0.25em]
\nolinkurl{FourierProduct.lean} & Entire sinc, pointwise multipliability, finite-product recurrence, and equality of the product with the analytic Fourier transform. \\
\nolinkurl{RvachevLaurentLeading.lean} & Exhaustive 1+6 centered-MGF/Fourier rotation and integer-cofactor limit API, including the manuscript's exact odd-core leading Laurent coefficient.  Limits are punctured because inversion is totalized at the pole; no lower Laurent coefficients are supplied. \\
\nolinkurl{GeneralizedRvachevIdentifiability.lean} & Zero definitions and six theorems recovering an admissible exponent sequence from dyadic analytic orders, with the exact generalized-product equality criterion. \\
\nolinkurl{FourierAnalytic.lean} & Entire Fourier transform of \(\RvachevUp\), Fourier inversion consequences, and its imaginary-axis relation to the half-moment generating function. \\
\nolinkurl{OriginalCharacterization.lean} & Rvachev's original compact-support predicate, derived scale positivity, the forced scale and normalization, and the general fold from bounded Fabius solutions. \\
\nolinkurl{OriginalUniqueness.lean} & Fourier-refinement uniqueness, the exact kernel of the fold, and the equivalences with fixed candidates or unique bounded Fabius witnesses. \\
\nolinkurl{WeightedUniformSeries.lean} & Absolutely summable uniform-coordinate series, their canonical pushforward laws, pointwise and law-level head--tail splitting, reflection, and carried-interval results. \\
\nolinkurl{WeightedUniformDistribution.lean} & Exact zero-definition/eleven-theorem compatibility API for weighted laws, including the generic half-total-weight barycenter, scalar transport, affine recursion, sharp absolute continuity from any nonzero real coefficient, and null singletons. \\
\nolinkurl{WeightedUniformSupport.lean} & Support equal to the continuous series range, exact signed-real min/max support intervals, and nonnegative unit-mass specializations. \\
\nolinkurl{ContinuousCDF.lean} & Generic continuity of atomless probability CDFs, reflection symmetry from measure invariance, and the theorem identifying any everywhere pointwise CDF derivative as the exact Lebesgue density without prior regularity or absolute continuity assumptions. \\
\nolinkurl{AffineIndependentCopy.lean} & The affine independent-copy convolution operator, its probability and product-map forms, characteristic-function factorization and finite fixed-point iteration, and contractive uniqueness.  The digit space is arbitrary measurable, the target is second-countable Borel real inner-product, and completeness is confined to the three uniqueness declarations; \(\lvert q\rvert<1\) includes zero and negative scales without support, density, or moment hypotheses. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricUniformLaw.lean} & Exhaustive 24-declaration API for the named weights \((1-q)q^n\), their series and probability law, exact mean \(1/2\) for \(\lvert q\rvert<1\) including zero and negative ratios, affine self-similarity, reflection, absolute continuity, null singletons, and exact range/support statements. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricUniformMultisection.lean} & Fixed normalized even/odd split of the half-ratio coordinate series into two independent quarter-ratio copies, with pointwise, product-map, and convolution forms. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricUniformUniqueness.lean} & Specializes the generic product-map theorem to prove \nolinkurl{eq_geometricUniformDistribution_of_selfSimilar}: every probability solution of the geometric affine head--tail equation is \(\mu_q\) when \(\lvert q\rvert<1\), including \(q=0\) and negative \(q\), with no support, density, or moment assumption. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricSincFactorization.lean} & Closed characteristic function of the scaled uniform digit, exact closed-factor and phase-collapsed finite residual formulas for \(\lvert q\rvert<1\), pointwise convergence of the phase-bearing sinc prefixes, and the two dyadic weighted-sum wrappers. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricReciprocalGamma.lean} & Locally uniform convergence, pointwise multipliability and \nolinkurl{HasProd}, and entireness of the geometric sinc product, together with its reciprocal-Gamma reflection factorization. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricSincCharacteristicFunction.lean} & The four-theorem, zero-definition surface: two exact phase-bearing bridges from the geometric-uniform characteristic function to the rescaled geometric sinc product and paired reciprocal-Gamma factors, plus locally uniform convergence of the full phase-bearing prefixes on the real frequency line and uniform convergence on every compact real frequency set, for every \(\lvert q\rvert<1\), including zero and negative ratios. \\
\nolinkurl{RvachevPochhammerFactorization.lean} & One definition and ten theorems: the total complex infinite Pochhammer product, its unconditional definitional equality with \nolinkurl{qPochhammerInfIn}, its strict-contraction convergence API, absolute summability and paired scale--zero product, the global \(S_q(z)=\prod_{k\ge0}(z^2/(k+1)^2;q^2)_\infty\) factorization for complex \(\lVert q\rVert<1\), and its two dyadic Rvachev/Fabius specializations. \\
\nolinkurl{QPochhammerEntire.lean} & Exactly five compatibility theorems and no public definitions: for each fixed complex \(\lVert q\rVert<1\), locally uniform convergence in \(a\), entireness of \(a\mapsto(a;q)_\infty\), the total factor-zero criterion, the reciprocal-power lattice when \(q\ne0\), and analytic order one at every zero under the historical complex-product name.  The generic analytic-order theorem is owned by \nolinkurl{QPochhammerInfinite.lean}; the \(q=0\)-safe statements remain division-free, and no joint holomorphy or centered/MGF wrapper is asserted. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricPochhammerNormalConvergence.lean} & Exactly three theorems and no public definitions: locally uniform convergence on the whole complex plane of the outer geometric-sinc Pochhammer product for every strict contraction \(\lVert q\rVert<1\), including \(q=0\), and its dyadic standalone-product and bounded-Fabius specializations. \\
\nolinkurl{QPochhammerDissection.lean} & Exactly two theorems and no public definitions: finite exact residue-class dissection over every commutative ring, and its remainder form under \(u\le r\), including all empty and \(u=r\) boundaries. \\
\nolinkurl{QPochhammerInfinite.lean} & One definition and exactly twenty-nine theorems: the total generic \(q\)-Pochhammer \texttt{tprod}; strict-contraction summability, multipliability, finite-prefix convergence, concatenation, factor removal and positive-modulus dissection in complete normed commutative rings; the exact factor-zero locus under a multiplicative norm; locally uniform convergence and continuity over complete locally compact normed fields; complex entireness, nonzero derivatives at raw factor zeros (including \(q=0\)), reciprocal-power derivative formulas for \(q\ne0\), and analytic order one at every zero. \\
\nolinkurl{QBinomialTheoremInfinite.lean} & One definition and exactly twenty-seven theorems: dominated passage from finite Gaussian coefficients, positivity and nonvanishing controls, quantitative finite-product and fixed-column estimates, the infinite q-binomial theorem, and Euler product and reciprocal expansions. \\
\nolinkurl{GaussianBinomialFixedColumnRate.lean} & No definitions and exactly nine theorems: two finite-product defect bounds, the denominator-free relative Gaussian bound, fixed and shifted nonasymptotic additive bounds, and four relative/additive geometric \nolinkurl{IsBigO} laws. \nolinkurl{EndpointTransferPolynomials.lean} & Universal endpoint-transfer polynomials over \(\RationalNumbers[X]\): recurrence, weighted-partition formula, exact formal exponential series, finite truncation, evaluation naturality, and the coefficients through order three. \\
\nolinkurl{PolynomialExpectationCumulant.lean} & Finite polynomial integrals as coefficient-weighted raw moments and complete Bell transforms of formal cumulants, including the exact zeroth-moment correction and the probability-measure specialization. \\

\nolinkurl{CompleteHomogeneousGenerating.lean} & Finite formal generating series for complete homogeneous evaluations: coefficient and adjoining-variable recurrences over commutative semirings, and one-factor/full-product denominator clearing plus the canonical \nolinkurl{PowerSeries.invOfUnit} reciprocal over commutative rings.  No analytic evaluation, convergence, or Bell/generalized-harmonic conversion is asserted. \\
\nolinkurl{CompleteHomogeneousBell.lean} & Finite power sums and factorially weighted Bell input; the division-free complete-homogeneous/complete-Bell identity over every commutative semiring and its factorially normalized pointwise report form over commutative \(\RationalNumbers\)-algebras.  No analytic convergence is asserted. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricCompleteHomogeneous.lean} & Denominator-free principal specialization \(h_n(1,q,\ldots,q^p)=\GaussianBinomial{n+p}{p}{q}\), its arbitrary common-scale form over every commutative semiring, and the range-indexed bridge used by interpolation rows. \\
\nolinkurl{LagrangeResidualMoments.lean} & Every higher residual moment of a polynomially exact finite row, with a representation-independent commutative-ring theorem and the distinct-node Lagrange specialization. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricResidualMoments.lean} & Exhaustive zero-definition/nine-theorem surface: every positive residual moment on \(c,cq,\ldots,cq^p\) over a commutative ring, assuming exactness directly on those scaled nodes, together with distinct-node Lagrange, arbitrary-dilation, shifted-block, and polynomial-exactness field corollaries.  The theorem \nolinkurl{sum_geometricLagrangeWeight_mul_eval_scaled_geometric} assumes finite power-node injectivity and allows arbitrary \(c\), making \nolinkurl{cor:scaled-geometric-moments} Exact by composition with the positive-moment theorem. \\
\nolinkurl{AnalyticSeriesFilter.lean} & Unconditional pointwise evaluation of finite coefficient filters: diagonalization under weighted sampled-series summability, exact finite heads and tails, and Gaussian residual tails for geometric and geometric-Lagrange rows, including shifted scales.  This generic module does not supply conditional boundary summation, uniform estimates, asymptotic rates, or a sinc-product instantiation. \\
\nolinkurl{RvachevQBinomialFilter.lean} & Exact specialization of the Gaussian filter to the actual infinite Rvachev product: the named even modes sum to the product and scale by powers of \(c^2\); the generic complex theorem assumes exactly finite-power injectivity, the \(c=1/2\), \(q=1/4\) theorem is assumption-free, and the model-generic wrapper applies to every bounded \nolinkurl{IsFabius} solution. \nolinkurl{QBinomialVandermonde.lean} & Both zero-extended \(q\)-Vandermonde orientations, full-range and exact-\(\min\)-support central forms, the total positive shifted-central identity, and two negative-shift forms under exactly \(k\leq N\), plus the total integer-index shifted-central identity and its finitely supported \nolinkurl{finsum} form built from \nolinkurl{gaussianBinomialInt}, all denominator-free over arbitrary commutative semirings. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricRichardson.lean} & Generic geometric root polynomials and normalized forward Richardson polynomials, including their evaluation and coefficient interfaces. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricLagrangeWeights.lean} & Identification of the geometric Lagrange row with the Richardson coefficient polynomial and its complete-homogeneous all-order moments. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricLagrangeQBinomial.lean} & Field-valued q-Pochhammer weight formulas and report-facing rational q-binomial and quarter-base coefficient formulas. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricLagrangeQMoments.lean} & Closed forms for every rational geometric moment under nonzero-base and nonvanishing finite-denominator hypotheses, with exact residual sign and finite total variation for \(0<q<1\) and dedicated quarter-base corollaries. \\
\nolinkurl{GeometricRichardsonGenerating.lean} & Exhaustive three-definition/seven-theorem surface. The first generating identity is formal over a commutative ring with totalized inverses; the exact geometric-Lagrange form assumes a field and nonzero nome; the two analytic \nolinkurl{HasSum} forms additionally require completeness, strict nome contraction, and absolute summability of the normalized data. \\

\nolinkurl{GeometricUniformMomentPolynomialBridge.lean} & Exhaustive zero-definition/one-theorem surface: \leansubtwo{geometricUniformMomentPolynomial_eval}{_eq_mgf_taylorCoefficient} identifies that polynomial with the normalized Taylor coefficient of the actual geometric-uniform MGF for every real \(\lvert q\rvert<1\), making \nolinkurl{p7:eq:Pn-def} Exact in that regime. \nolinkurl{RvachevLagrangeNodesOnly.lean} & Exhaustive one-definition/fourteen-theorem surface named in the crosswalk: ordinary-derivative and raw omitted-node Appell forms, rational descent and rational lattice samples, and formal complete-Bell/Bernoulli--Mersenne coefficients. \nolinkurl{ThueMorseGammaTowerDifferential.lean} & No definitions and exactly three theorems, named in the crosswalk: positive-parameter Mellin differentiation and the full finite GammaLog derivative ladder. \nolinkurl{GeometricUniformComplexMomentProduct.lean} & Exhaustive one-definition/three-theorem surface: \nolinkurl{geometricUniformComplexMomentProduct} is the actual complex product, \nolinkurl{hasProdLocallyUniformly_geometricUniformComplexMomentProduct} proves its locally uniform convergence for \(\lVert q\rVert<1\), \nolinkurl{differentiable_geometricUniformComplexMomentProduct} proves complex differentiability on the whole plane, and \nolinkurl{geometricUniformMomentPolynomial_eval}\texttt{\textsubscript{2}}\nolinkurl{_eq_complexMomentProduct_taylorCoefficient} gives the normalized Taylor-coefficient bridge. \nolinkurl{GeometricUniformMomentPolynomialDegree.lean} & Exhaustive zero-definition/three-theorem surface: \nolinkurl{coeff_geometricUniformMomentPolynomial_choose_two} and \nolinkurl{coeff_geometricUniformMomentPolynomial_choose_two_sub_one} give the top and subleading Bernoulli coefficients, while \nolinkurl{geometricUniformMomentPolynomial_natDegree_eq} gives the exact parity-sensitive degree. \nolinkurl{GeometricUniformMomentRatFunc.lean} & Exhaustive one-definition/four-theorem surface: \nolinkurl{geometricUniformMomentRatFunc}; \nolinkurl{qFactorial_mul_geometricUniformMomentRatFunc}; \nolinkurl{eval_geometricUniformMomentRatFunc_eq_complexMomentProduct_taylorCoefficient}; \nolinkurl{eval_geometricUniformMomentRatFunc_eq_exteriorComplexMomentGerm_taylorCoefficient}; and \nolinkurl{eval_geometricUniformMomentRatFunc_one}. \nolinkurl{GeometricUniformMomentReciprocity.lean} & Exhaustive one-definition/five-theorem surface: \nolinkurl{geometricUniformComplexMomentGerm}; \nolinkurl{geometricUniformComplexMomentGerm_of_norm_lt_one}; \nolinkurl{geometricUniformComplexMomentGerm_of_one_lt_norm}; \nolinkurl{analyticAt_geometricUniformComplexMomentGerm}; \nolinkurl{geometricUniformComplexMomentGerm_reciprocity}; and \nolinkurl{geometricUniformComplexMomentGerm_moment_convolution}. \nolinkurl{PowerExponentialLambertCurvature.lean} & Four regular-interior derivative-of-derivative and exact second-derivative declarations for the two generic scaled phases; no generic sign, threshold, or shape package. \\
\nolinkurl{PowerExponentialLambertInverse.lean} & Exact images and \nolinkurl{InvOn} laws, classification of every nonnegative root below or at the peak, and strict-interior separation of the two roots. \\

\begin{remark}
Some helper files are intentionally absent from this table, and a few families may acquire additional leaves as the repository evolves.  The umbrella module and the actual import graph remain the definitive inventory.  Duplicate-looking module names usually mark distinct proof stages---for example, raw algebra versus scalar specialization, or a central estimate versus its all-order strengthening.

ordinary Bell normalization, and the Touchard definition and displayed
Euler-operator equation are Exact.  The real basic Wright omega row
\nolinkurl{plt:prop:mot-omega-basic} is also Exact, including real analyticity,
without asserting complex holomorphy.  The broader Wright omega claims,
differential closure,
harmonic increments, Cayley, derangement, Lambert correction and its bracket
bounds, linear-logarithmic inversion, power-logarithmic inversion, remainder
transport, and staircase inversion remain Partial; the least-term-index row
is Neighboring.  Every module is reachable from the facade.  No aggregate facade

remain historical receipts and do not claim parity with the merged source; no
PDF was built for this source-only union.

The exhaustive public surface of
\nolinkurl{FinitePrefixThueMorseCollapse.lean} is zero definitions and eight
theorems:
\lean{Appell.sum_thueMorseSign_mul_eval_poly},
\lean{Fabius.sum_thueMorseSign_mul_uncenteredDyadicPrefixAppellPolynomialRat},
\lean{Fabius.sum_thueMorseSign_mul_uncenteredDyadicPrefixAppellPolynomialRat_of_lt},
\lean{Fabius.sum_thueMorseSign_mul_uncenteredDyadicPrefixAppellPolynomialRat_self},
\lean{Fabius.sum_thueMorseSign_mul_centeredDyadicPrefixAppellPolynomialRat},
\lean{Fabius.sum_thueMorseSign_mul_centeredDyadicPrefixAppellPolynomialRat_succ},
\lean{Fabius.sum_thueMorseSign_mul_centeredDyadicPrefixAppellPolynomialRat_of_lt},
and
\lean{Fabius.sum_thueMorseSign_mul_centeredDyadicPrefixAppellPolynomialRat_self}.
Writing \(A^{\mathrm{unc}}_{N,n}\) and \(A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{N,n}\) for the
two rational prefix Appell polynomials and \(s_N=1-2^{-N}\), their complete
signed responses are
\[
 \sum_{k<2^N}\varepsilon_k
   A^{\mathrm{unc}}_{N,n}(x+k/2^N)
 =(-1)^N2^{-\binom{N+1}{2}}n^{\underline{N}}x^{n-N}
\]
and
\[
 \sum_{k<2^N}\varepsilon_k
   A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{N,n}(x+s_N-2k/2^N)
 =2^{-\binom{N}{2}}n^{\underline{N}}x^{n-N}.
\]
Thus the uncentered response has the exact sign and
\(2^{-\binom{N+1}{2}}\) normalization, while the centered response is
sign-free with \(2^{-\binom{N}{2}}\).  For positive depth \(N=m+1\), the
successor theorem uses the literal manuscript grid
\(x+s_{m+1}-k/2^m\).  The two \texttt{of\_lt} theorems give cancellation
below the block depth, and the two \texttt{self} theorems give the first
nonzero constants.  These results make
\nolinkurl{is:p2:thm:TM-uncentered},
\nolinkurl{is:p2:cor:Prouhet-canonical}, and
\nolinkurl{is:p2:thm:TM-centered} Exact by composition.  The total main
theorems include \(N=0\), strengthening the positive-depth formulas.  The
entire leaf is rational coefficient algebra: it introduces no random
variable or \lean{HasLaw}, proves no analytic MGF, and makes no Barnes-function
identification.  The three promotions move the 194-row inverse concordance to
57 Lean-proved, 88 human-proved, 10 conjectural, 15 open, and 24
nonassertoric rows.

Those six counts are \nolinkurl{QPochhammerInfiniteBounds.lean} (0+5),
\nolinkurl{HeineTransformation.lean} (2+5),
\nolinkurl{QGaussSummation.lean} (0+2),
\nolinkurl{QPochhammerComplexOrder.lean} (1+4),
\nolinkurl{BasicHypergeometricSeries.lean} (2+5), and
\nolinkurl{QMultinomial.lean} (1+9).
The four subsequent counts are
\nolinkurl{GaussianBinomialPalindromic.lean} (0+14),
\nolinkurl{JacksonIntegral.lean} (1+7),
\nolinkurl{QExponential.lean} (3+8), and
\nolinkurl{ThetaQuasiPeriodicity.lean} (1+6).  They add Gaussian-polynomial
degree, palindromicity, and the total linear-coefficient classifier,
q-exponential and q-derivative laws, Jackson
integration, and bilateral-theta quasi-periodicity and zeros under the
displayed analytic hypotheses.
The four newest counts are
\nolinkurl{GaussianBinomialPolynomialStructure.lean} (0+5),
\nolinkurl{JacobiCubic.lean} (0+2),
\nolinkurl{QPochhammerLogDerivative.lean} (0+10), and
\nolinkurl{QPochhammerOrderDerivative.lean} (0+3).  Their twenty theorems add
universal Gaussian polynomial structure, Jacobi's cubic identity, the
q-Pochhammer logarithmic derivative and Lambert-series form, and differentiation
with respect to complex order under the displayed hypotheses.
The final two counts are
\nolinkurl{CentralQBinomialReduction.lean} (0+6) and
\nolinkurl{CyclotomicFactorization.lean} (0+7).  Their thirteen theorems add
finite-symbol sign pairing and even--odd dissection, the central Gaussian
reduction, and cyclotomic factorizations.  The quotient and integral-domain
hypotheses remain explicit.
The four root-of-unity and q-Catalan counts are
\nolinkurl{CyclotomicDivisibility.lean} (0+3),
\nolinkurl{PrimitiveRootBlock.lean} (0+3),
\nolinkurl{QCatalan.lean} (1+11), and
\nolinkurl{QLucas.lean} (0+7).  Their one definition and twenty-four theorems
add the cyclotomic carry criterion, primitive-root blocks, q-Lucas, and the

The newest two counts are
\nolinkurl{QBetaIntegral.lean} (1+8) and
\nolinkurl{NewtonInterpolation.lean} (3+19).  Their four definitions and

differences, the explicit geometric-grid specialization, and the seven-name
\nolinkurl{newtonInterpolant} compatibility surface.  The q-beta
theorems retain \(0<q<1\) and positivity of both arguments.
The latest three counts are
\nolinkurl{GaussianBinomialInteger.lean} (1+10),
\nolinkurl{GaussianBinomialComplexOrder.lean} (1+5), and
\nolinkurl{QPfaffSaalschutz.lean} (0+3).  Their two definitions and eighteen
theorems extend Gaussian coefficients to integer and principal-branch complex
upper indices, prove the two integer q-Pascal laws and negative-index
reflection, give the reciprocal finite and generalized complex-order
q-binomial series, and prove the terminating balanced \({}_3\phi_2\)
q-Pfaff--Saalsch\"utz sum.  All nonzero, strict-contraction, and denominator

The terminating reversal pair contributes
\nolinkurl{TwoPhiOneReversal.lean} (2+12) and
\nolinkurl{QChuVandermonde.lean} (0+10), with the public

The subsequent \nolinkurl{JacobiTwoSquareCount.lean}
leaf is 0+4 and closes the nonzero two-square count and both Lambert forms;

The final zero-definition/five-theorem
\nolinkurl{QuantumMultinomial.lean} module decomposes tuple antidiagonals,

coefficient commutation, and expands powers of a finite sum into ordered

the nome with every variable.  It is finite and division-free.
The final \nolinkurl{GaussianBinomialBounds.lean} count is 0+6:
\nolinkurl{gaussianBinomial_inv}, \nolinkurl{one_le_gaussianBinomial},
\nolinkurl{finiteQPochhammerIn_pow_le_one},
\nolinkurl{gaussianBinomial_le_inv_qPochhammerInfIn},

The final terminating-basic-hypergeometric pair is
\nolinkurl{TwoPhiOneReversal.lean} (2+12) and

theorems are \nolinkurl{choose_two_add_succ_choose_two},
\nolinkurl{finiteQPochhammerIn_sub_eq},
\nolinkurl{finiteQPochhammerIn_reversal_ne_zero},
\nolinkurl{finiteQPochhammerIn_inv_pow_self},
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOneReflection_involutive},
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOneFinite_reversal},
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOneFinite_reversal_twice},
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOneFinite_eq_sum_twoPhiOneTerm},
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOne_eq_twoPhiOneFinite_inv_pow},
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOne_reversal}, \nolinkurl{twoPhiOne_reversal_twice}, and
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOne_one_eq_twoPhiOneFinite_zero}.  Thus it connects the
finite terminating sum to the actual \nolinkurl{twoPhiOne} tsum and proves
that the parameter reflection and the full reversal are involutive.

The ten theorems of \nolinkurl{QChuVandermonde.lean} are
\nolinkurl{two_mul_choose_two}, \nolinkurl{mul_sub_one_eq_mul_sub_add},
\nolinkurl{finiteQPochhammerIn_div_eq_sum_chu},
\nolinkurl{q_chu_vandermonde_first},
\nolinkurl{finiteQPochhammerIn_div_eq_sum_chu_second},
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOneFinite_mul_finiteQPochhammerIn_eq_chu_second},
\nolinkurl{q_chu_vandermonde_second},
\nolinkurl{q_chu_vandermonde_second_by_reversal},
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOne_q_chu_vandermonde_first}, and
\nolinkurl{twoPhiOne_q_chu_vandermonde_second}.  They prove both
q-Chu--Vandermonde evaluations, their actual-tsum wrappers, a
denominator-cleared second identity, and a separately named reversal route.
Thus the two displayed q-Chu evaluations and the reversal lemma are exact.
The claim that reversal alone yields the second identity over its full
displayed domain remains partial: the compiled by-reversal theorem assumes
\(C\ne0\) and \((A;q)_n\ne0\), while the unrestricted denominator-defined
theorem is obtained directly from finite q-Cauchy.  Rational continuation and
a commutative-ring cleared extension are not claimed.

The three later additions to
\nolinkurl{GaussianBinomialCumulants.lean}, now 2+24, are
\nolinkurl{eval_one_derivative_derivative_gaussianBinomial_X},
\nolinkurl{twelve_mul_secondMoment_gaussianBinomial_eval_one}, and
\nolinkurl{twelve_mul_varianceNumerator_gaussianBinomial_eval_one}.  For
\(P=[n,k]_X\), \(D=k(n-k)\), and \(C=\binom nk\), the first assumes a
characteristic-zero field and exactly \(k\le n\), and proves
\(P''(1)=D(3D+n-5)C/12\).  The other two hold for every \(n,k\) over an
arbitrary commutative semiring and state
\[
12\bigl(P''(1)+P'(1)\bigr)=D(3D+n+1)C,
\qquad
12C\bigl(P''(1)+P'(1)\bigr)=12P'(1)^2+D(n+1)C^2.
\]
They use no division, nonvanishing, or characteristic hypothesis, are total
above the Gaussian row by zero extension, and include the zero and diagonal
rows.  The first cleared identity is the raw second moment; the second is the
variance numerator.
\end{remark}

\section{Corrections and critical distinctions}\label{app:corrections}

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.25\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.32\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.349\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Topic & Informal hazard & Formal treatment \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
Topic & Informal hazard & Formal treatment \\
\midrule
\endhead
Bounded versus global \(F\) & A translation or dyadic formula is written for a clamped function on all of \(\RealNumbers\). & Use \nolinkurl{extendedFabius} for the signed global statement; prove agreement with the bounded function on the unit interval. \\
Original finite convolution & A limiting convolution object is used where a finite-stage identity is required. & State the convolution theorem for each finite row, then pass to the limit through weak convergence. \\
Step endpoints & Closed interval indicators count a shared endpoint twice. & Define a half-weight endpoint representative when the theorem is pointwise; retain ordinary indicators for almost-everywhere/integral statements. \\
Fourier equation factor & A transform variable or scaling factor is dropped during differentiation/dilation. & Derive the identity from mathlib's Fourier derivative and affine-map theorems under a fixed convention. \\
Poisson equations~(25) and~(32) & Equation~(25) omits the translation variable from the exponential, equation~(32) retains a factor \(1/a\) after the equation has been scaled by \(a\), and the printed specialization carries an unused upper bound \(a\leq1\). & Use the proved normalized identity with \(e^{2\pi\ImaginaryUnit kt/u}\); after multiplying both sides by the spacing, cancel the reciprocal factor.  The sharp support specialization needs only \(a\geq\tfrac12\). \\
Positive index & A denominator, derivative order, or truncated product is used at \(n=0\) although the formula assumes \(n>0\). & Add the explicit order hypothesis and prove the zero case separately when meaningful. \\
Removable singularity & A quotient such as \((e^z-1)/z\) is treated as globally defined. & Define a total function with the limiting value at zero and prove a global power-series theorem. \\
Dyadic representation & A formula is proved only for a reduced numerator even though later sums produce unreduced fractions. & Prove refinement invariance and formulate global correctness for arbitrary representatives. \\
Taylor series & Exact finite Taylor truncation is mistaken for local analyticity. & Use a finite-order Taylor theorem with vanishing remainder; separately prove the analytic-at predicate fails. \\
Logarithmic delay equation & A branch-dependent derivative law is differentiated without a domain. & State the transformed identity only for \(t\geq1\) and use eventual filters thereafter. \\
Periodic asymptotic term & A bounded log-periodic correction is absorbed into an \(o(1)\) or inverse-log error. & Prove the correction is nonconstant and exhibit phase subsequences with different residual limits. \\
Binary-reduction series & The outer sum begins after the endpoint contribution. & Start at \(m=0\) or include the missing boundary term explicitly so finite telescoping is exact. \\
Finite q dependence & Independence of the limiting value is promoted to independence of every approximant. & Preserve the complex q parameter at finite depth; prove its effect tends to zero through complex-shift estimates. \\
Theorem 20 naturality & A proof-internal sentence asserts that \(d_kN_n\) is natural. & The corrected integral quantity is \(2d_kN_n\); \nolinkurl{two_mul_halfMoment_mul_reshetnikovMultiplier_isNatural} and its oddness strengthening retain the missing factor \(2\). \\
Saddle expansion composition & A truncated Lambert expansion is substituted to all orders without a composition remainder proof. & State the primary all-order theorem at the exact phase and prove elementary-log expansions separately with implicit-function error bounds. \\
Quotient exponential fit & Numerical agreement over a finite range is treated as asymptotic equivalence. & Compare logarithmic scales and prove the proposed quotient has the wrong endpoint decay. \\
K-fold normalization & A draft normalization or global error is assumed from plots or low-depth data. & Evaluate exact small cases and formalize counterexamples before proving a repaired statement. \\
K-fold polygon & Convergence of corrected grid samples is restated as convergence of the literal interpolating polygon. & The literal polygon is refuted at \(x=1\); only the normalized grid samples are identified with moving-center step approximants and proved convergent. \\
K-fold local error & The proposed \(4h\) error bound is inferred from a picture. & At \(k=2,j=1\), the proposed slope is \(-2\) while \(F'(1/4)=1\), giving error \(3>4h=1\). \\
K-fold equation~(7) & An unbounded proxy sequence is assigned a finite maximum. & For every \(x>0\), its successive-ratio analysis gives growth like \(x2^{n+1}/(n+1)\), so no displayed maximum exists. \\
K-fold equation~(10) & A linear residual is hidden inside \(O(\log n)\). & Substituting the printed stationary equation leaves an explicit \(+n\) term, which cannot be absorbed into a logarithmic error. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\subsection{What these corrections teach}
The corrections fall into four recurring classes.  Domain errors arise when a local functional equation is used globally.  Representation errors arise when a bounded object is substituted for a signed extension or an almost-everywhere representative is substituted in a pointwise claim.  Normalization errors arise from Fourier, convolution, and dyadic scaling conventions.  Asymptotic errors arise when a bounded oscillation is mistaken for a small remainder or when a formal series is composed without analytic control.

Lean detects each class differently.  A domain error appears as an unprovable interval side condition.  A representation error appears as a concrete endpoint contradiction.  A normalization error leaves an unmatched scalar factor.  An asymptotic error usually survives local algebra but fails at the limit theorem; the repository then proves a negative result by constructing a subsequence or comparing scales.

\section{Lean and mathematical glossary}\label{app:glossary}

\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.25\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.68\textwidth}}
\toprule
Term or identifier & Meaning in this development \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
Term or identifier & Meaning in this development \\
\midrule
\endhead
\nolinkurl{UnitInterval} & The subtype \(\{x:\RealNumbers\mid0\leq x\leq1\}\), used as the codomain of the bounded function. \\
\nolinkurl{BoundedFabius} & A real-input function whose values carry the unit-interval bound in their type. \\
\nolinkurl{fabiusReal} & Real-valued projection of a subtype-valued bounded function, suitable for calculus and integration. \\
\nolinkurl{IsFabius} & Abstract specification combining tail values, smoothness, symmetry, and the left-half derivative law. \\
\nolinkurl{fabius} / \nolinkurl{fabius_spec} & The canonical constructed object and the theorem that it satisfies the specification. \\
\nolinkurl{rvachevUp} & Compactly supported even twin used in Fourier and probability arguments. \\
\nolinkurl{extendedFabius} & Signed globally defined extension assembled from Thue--Morse-weighted translates of the up-function. \\
\nolinkurl{HasDerivAt} & Pointwise derivative proposition carrying differentiability and the derivative value together. \\
\nolinkurl{ContDiff} & Smoothness predicate to finite or infinite order. \\
\nolinkurl{tsum} & Infinite sum over a type; translate sums are proved locally finite before this is manipulated. \\
\nolinkurl{HasSum} & Proposition that a series has a specified sum; often more convenient than rewriting a \nolinkurl{tsum}. \\
\nolinkurl{Finset.range n} & Finite set \(\{0,\dots,n-1\}\), the dominant indexing object for recurrences and finite products. \\
\nolinkurl{Nat.strong_induction_on} & Well-founded induction giving hypotheses for every smaller natural index. \\
\nolinkurl{IsBigO}, \nolinkurl{IsLittleO} & Filter-based asymptotic bounds and negligibility. \\
\nolinkurl{atTop} & Filter describing a variable tending to positive infinity. \\
\nolinkurl{nhdsWithin 0 (Set.Ioi 0)} & Right-hand approach to zero, the endpoint filter used for \(x\downarrow0\). \\
\nolinkurl{eventually} & A property holding on some set belonging to a filter; used to encode large-variable thresholds. \\
\nolinkurl{Summable} & Unconditional summability in the relevant normed additive group.  In \nolinkurl{AnalyticSeriesFilter.lean} the generic hypothesis is on each weighted sampled series; specialized geometric statements require an unweighted sample only when its weight is nonzero. \\
\nolinkurl{intervalIntegral} & Oriented integral over real endpoints, useful for FTC and integration by parts. \\
\nolinkurl{MeasureTheory.Integrable} & Whole-space integrability; usually proved from compact support and continuity. \\
\nolinkurl{SchwartzMap} & Smooth rapidly decreasing function type used for Fourier injectivity and Poisson summation. \\
\nolinkurl{fabiusDyadicValue} & Executable exact rational evaluator for a dyadic input representation. \\
\nolinkurl{fabiusDyadicUnitAux} & Well-founded highest-bit recursion at the core of unit-interval evaluation. \\
\nolinkurl{halfMoment} / \nolinkurl{moment} & Exact rational recurrence sequences later identified with analytic integrals. \\
\nolinkurl{halfMomentNumerator} / \nolinkurl{momentNumerator} & Division-free natural recurrences used for parity and denominator proofs. \\
\nolinkurl{binaryWeight} & Number of one-bits of a natural number. \\
\nolinkurl{thueMorseSign} & Sign \((-1)^{\BinaryDigitSum(n)}\). \\
\nolinkurl{halfQBinomial} & Exact q-binomial coefficient specialized to \(q=1/2\). \\
\nolinkurl{field_simp} & Tactic for clearing field denominators after nonvanishing hypotheses have been supplied. \\
\nolinkurl{norm_cast} / \nolinkurl{push_cast} & Tactics normalizing canonical casts across algebraic operations. \\
\nolinkurl{ring_nf} & Polynomial normalizer used after divisions, casts, and finite sums are brought to algebraic normal form. \\
\nolinkurl{filter_upwards} & Tactic for collecting eventual hypotheses and proving a pointwise eventual conclusion. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\section{Example exploration snippets}\label{app:snippets}

The following snippets are intentionally schematic where theorem argument order may change.  They show the style of interaction rather than replacing editor inspection of the current declarations.

\subsection{Inspect the public objects}
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction

open Fabius

#check UnitInterval
#check BoundedFabius
#check IsFabius
#check fabius
#check fabius_spec
#check rvachevUp
#check extendedFabius
\end{lstlisting}

\subsection{Evaluate exact dyadic data}
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction.Arithmetic

open Fabius

#eval fabiusInversePowTwoTable 8
#eval fabiusDyadicValue 1 1
#eval fabiusDyadicValue 5 4
#eval extendedFabiusDyadicValue 13 5
\end{lstlisting}
The returned objects are exact rationals.  To evaluate a rational input through the recognition layer, inspect the declarations near \nolinkurl{IsDyadicRational}, \nolinkurl{dyadicExponent?}, and the evaluator wrappers in \nolinkurl{Arithmetic.lean}.

\subsection{Work generically under the specification}
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction.Differential

open Fabius

variable (F : BoundedFabius) (hF : IsFabius F)

#check hF.symmetry_all
-- Search within Differential.lean for the iterated derivative
-- theorem whose interval hypotheses fit the desired dyadic cell.
\end{lstlisting}
The generic style is useful for proving a new consequence once and applying it both to the canonical function and to any future equivalent construction.

\subsection{Use the normalized Volterra foundation (post-snapshot)}
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction.NormalizedVolterra

open Fabius

#check iteratedPrimitive_add
#check iteratedPrimitive_succ_hasStrictDerivAt
#check iteratedPrimitive_eq_normalizedVolterra
#check normalizedVolterra_affine
#check normalizedVolterra_comp_affine
#check normalizedVolterra_basepoint_shift
#check normalizedVolterra_succ_eq_taylor_of_eq_zero
#check normalizedVolterra_succ_hasStrictDerivAt
#check iteratedDeriv_normalizedVolterra_add
#check contDiff_normalizedVolterra
#check normalizedVolterra_add
#check normalizedVolterra_succ_iteratedDeriv_eq_sub_taylor
#check normalizedVolterra_polynomial
#check normalizedVolterra_monomial
\end{lstlisting}
This focused import is independent of the Fabius specification.  Its operators
have arbitrary base points and oriented endpoints.  The division-free affine
law has no integrability hypothesis and includes negative and zero scales; its
inverse form asks only that the scale be nonzero.  The base-point shift assumes
interval integrability on its two oriented pieces, while the positive-order
zero-tail form asks for integrability only up to the new base point and vanishing
on the open interval beyond it.  All four work in a real normed space.  Only the
continuous literal-iterator/strict-FTC bridge, derivative cancellation and
smoothness, and Taylor reconstruction need completeness.
Specialize only when the Fabius dilation
law is actually needed:
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction.FabiusAntiderivatives

open Fabius

variable (F : BoundedFabius) (hF : IsFabius F)

#check normalizedVolterra_extendedFabius F hF
#check normalizedVolterra_fabiusReal_of_le_one F hF
#check normalizedVolterra_pow_mul_extendedFabius F hF
#check normalizedVolterra_pow_mul_fabiusReal_of_le_one F hF
\end{lstlisting}
The signed-extension theorems have unrestricted real endpoints; the bounded
theorems use the exact hypothesis \(x\le1\).  The generic affine and base-point
API does not yet give named Fabius-specific declarations for the \(\RvachevUp\) ladder
based at \(-1\), the reflected \(1-F\) ladder, arbitrary affine copies of \(F\),
or the full piecewise polynomial tail beyond the support of \(\RvachevUp\); those remain
separate frontier targets rather than additional \texttt{\#check} entries here.

\newpage
\subsection{Find source-facing arithmetic theorems}
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction.Paper06487

open Fabius

#check proposition_one
#check theorem_seven
#check theorem_twenty
#check theorem_twenty_one
#check conjecture_sixteen
\end{lstlisting}
A declaration representing a conjecture is a definition or proposition describing the assertion, not a proof of it.

\subsection{Inspect asymptotic interfaces}
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction.PaperFabiusAsymptotic

open Fabius

-- Use #check on the named main, obstruction, and full-expansion
-- theorems, then follow their exact-phase helper declarations.
#check Fabius.log_fabius_sub_correctedWikipediaMain_isBigO
#check Fabius.log_fabius_sub_WikipediaElementaryMain_not_isBigO
#check Fabius.log_fabius_dyadicReal_sub_sharpLambertExpansion_isBigO
\end{lstlisting}
Because filter statements can be long, editor hover information is often more useful than printing the entire type in a terminal.

\newpage
\subsection{Search tactics}
Within an editor, useful text searches include:
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
IsFabius -> HasDerivAt
fabiusDyadicValue -> Real
rvachevUp -> Fourier
negativeLaplaceLog
HasAsymptoticExpansion
Legendre -> HasSum
\end{lstlisting}
Searching by the transition one wants---rather than by a source theorem number---usually finds the strong internal lemma.

\section{Reproduction and trust checks}\label{app:reproduction}

\subsection{Pin the inspected tree}
The original walkthrough baseline was checked against the full base commit
printed on the title page, not against an unspecified moving branch.  This
merged edition preserves that recipe as a historical baseline check; it is not
a validation recipe for the later merged closures.  To reproduce the original
baseline, begin with:
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo,language={}]
git rev-parse HEAD
source_pin=eaea1b9afe2b7ff0b7dd880bd1710e303dec6d80
test "$(git rev-parse "$source_pin^{commit}")" = "$source_pin"
git diff --exit-code "$source_pin" -- \
  Analysis/FabiusFunction/Lean
git show -s --format=%cs "$source_pin"
lake env lean --version
rg --files Analysis/FabiusFunction/Lean/FabiusFunction \
  -g '*.lean' | wc -l
\end{lstlisting}
At the pinned snapshot the last command reports \(189\) leaf modules.  A
different count is not automatically an error, but it means this guide's
module inventory must be compared with the newer umbrella import.  Do not run
\texttt{lake update}: the committed manifest is part of the audited
environment.

\subsection{Build and inspect trust}
The release-level library check is
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo,language={}]
LAKE_JOBS=1 lake build +FabiusFunction
\end{lstlisting}
The repository policy requires one prefixed module target per Lake invocation,
with \texttt{LAKE\_JOBS=1}, including during debugging, and forbids setting
\texttt{LEAN\_NUM\_THREADS=0}.  It also requires a source audit for declarations that bypass
ordinary proof checking:
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo,language={}]
rg -n '\b(sorry|admit)\b' \
  Analysis/FabiusFunction/Lean -g '*.lean'
rg -n '\b(axiom|opaque)\b' \
  Analysis/FabiusFunction/Lean -g '*.lean'
\end{lstlisting}
The first search is empty at the pinned snapshot.  The second intentionally
shows two comment-only uses of the word \emph{opaque}; inspect the raw matches
to confirm that neither is a declaration.  For representative public theorems, a small
scratch file may additionally request:
\par\noindent
\begin{minipage}{\textwidth}
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo]
import FabiusFunction

#print axioms Fabius.fabiusDyadicValue_cast
#print axioms Fabius.log_fabius_sub_correctedWikipediaMain_isBigO
\end{lstlisting}
\end{minipage}
\par
Any reported axioms must be drawn from the repository's permitted set
\nolinkurl{propext}, \nolinkurl{Classical.choice}, and \nolinkurl{Quot.sound}; an individual
theorem may use only a subset.

\subsection{Rebuild this document}
From this document's directory, use exactly three passes:
\begin{lstlisting}[style=pseudo,language={}]
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error \
  fabius_lean_walkthrough.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error \
  fabius_lean_walkthrough.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error \
  fabius_lean_walkthrough.tex
rm -f fabius_lean_walkthrough.aux \
  fabius_lean_walkthrough.log \
  fabius_lean_walkthrough.out \
  fabius_lean_walkthrough.toc
\end{lstlisting}
The source and generated PDF belong in the same commit.  A successful TeX
build checks syntax and references, but it does not validate mathematical
prose; that is why this walkthrough also cites exact declaration names and
records its nonclaims.
